{"forms":[{"form":"Pauli matrixes","tags":["plural"]},{"form":"Pauli matrices","tags":["plural"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"noun","senses":[{"examples":[{"text":"The Pauli matrices satisfy the equation #92;sigma#95;1²#61;#92;sigma#95;2²#61;#92;sigma#95;3²#61;-i#92;sigma#95;1#92;sigma#95;2#92;sigma#95;3#61;I which is reminiscent of Hamilton's fundamental formula for quaternion multiplication.","type":"example"},{"text":"The first Pauli matrix is like a reflection about the \"y=x\" line. The third Pauli matrix is like a reflection about the \"x axis\". The second Pauli matrix is like a 90° counterclockwise rotation and scalar multiplication by the imaginary unit (which rotates the two complex components by 90° counterclockwise in their own \"phasor spaces\", as it were).","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Any one of three complex matrices that arise in Wolfgang Pauli's treatment of spin in quantum mechanics."],"topics":["mathematics","sciences"]}],"upstream_line":2298554,"upstream_offset":7110249001,"upstream_record_sha256":"544e387a6381a8153a9ab3ad68b4754c77bae98cf02229b2a7f3f67358fb4775","word":"Pauli matrix"}
